which satisfies the lower bound (36) for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F \in {\mathcal {H}}^1_0$$\end{document}F∈H01.

Proof

We use the bound \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$| \langle A v, w \rangle _{V^{*}, V} | \leqslant C \Vert v \Vert _{\dot{V}} \Vert w \Vert _{\dot{V}}$$\end{document}|⟨Av,w⟩V∗,V|⩽C‖v‖V˙‖w‖V˙ together with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to obtain the claimed upper bound for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert (1 -\mathcal {L}_0) F \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^{- 1}_0}$$\end{document}‖(1-L0)F‖H0-1 by testing against \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G \in {\mathcal {C}}$$\end{document}G∈C:37\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E} [(1 -\mathcal {L}_0) F G]&= \mathbb {E} [F G] +\mathbb {E} [\delta (A D F) G] \nonumber \\&= \mathbb {E} [F G] +\mathbb {E} [\langle A D F, D G \rangle _H] \nonumber \\&\leqslant \mathbb {E} [F^2]^{1 / 2} \mathbb {E} [G^2]^{1 / 2} + C\mathbb {E} [\Vert D F \Vert _{\dot{V}}^2]^{1 / 2} \mathbb {E} [\Vert D G \Vert _{\dot{V}}]^{1 / 2} \nonumber \\&\lesssim \Vert F \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^1_0} \Vert G \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^1_0} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[(1-L0)FG]=E[FG]+E[δ(ADF)G]=E[FG]+E[⟨ADF,DG⟩H]⩽E[F2]1/2E[G2]1/2+CE[‖DF‖V˙2]1/2E[‖DG‖V˙]1/2≲‖F‖H01‖G‖H01.For the lower bound, we similarly estimate\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E} [((1 -\mathcal {L}_0) F) F]&= \mathbb {E} [F^2] +\mathbb {E} [\langle A D F, D F \rangle _H] \nonumber \\&\geqslant \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[((1-L0)F)F]=E[F2]+E[⟨ADF,DF⟩H]⩾E[F2]+cE[‖DF‖V˙2]≳‖F‖H012.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} Definition 2.13

(\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V \otimes _s H$$\end{document}V⊗sH) For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}m∈N, symmetric \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$a = (a_{i j})_{i, j = 1, \ldots , m} \in \mathbb {R}^{m \times m}$$\end{document}a=(aij)i,j=1,…,m∈Rm×m, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_1, \ldots , v_m \in V$$\end{document}v1,…,vm∈V we define\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \left\| \sum _{i, j = 1}^m a_{i j} v_i \otimes v_j \right\| _{V \otimes _s H}^2 :=\sum _{i_1, j_1, i_2, j_2 = 1}^m a_{i_1 j_1} a_{i_2 j_2} \langle v_{i_1}, v_{i_2} \rangle _V \langle v_{j_1}, v_{j_2} \rangle _H, $$\end{document}∑i,j=1maijvi⊗vjV⊗sH2:=∑i1,j1,i2,j2=1mai1j1ai2j2⟨vi1,vi2⟩V⟨vj1,vj2⟩H,and we write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} for the completion of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \begin{document}$$\Vert \cdot \Vert _{V \otimes _s H}$$\end{document}‖·‖V⊗sH.

In the following, we will strongly rely on our assumption that the map38\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} V \otimes _s H \ni \sum _{i, j = 1}^m a_{i j} v_i \otimes v_j \mapsto B \left( \sum _{i, j = 1}^m a_{i j} v_i \otimes v_j \right) :=\sum _{i, j = 1}^m a_{i j} B (v_i, v_j) \in \dot{V}^{*} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}V⊗sH∋∑i,j=1maijvi⊗vj↦B∑i,j=1maijvi⊗vj:=∑i,j=1maijB(vi,vj)∈V˙∗has a unique continuous extension to all of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V \otimes _s H$$\end{document}V⊗sH, which we still denote by B. (Uniform bounds for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {G}^\rho $$\end{document}Gρ) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho : H \rightarrow V$$\end{document}ρ:H→V be a bounded operator. Lemma 2.15

The following identity holds for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F \in \mathcal {C}$$\end{document}F∈C:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathbb {E} [\Vert D^2 F \Vert _{\dot{V} \otimes _s H}^2] = \Vert \boldsymbol{1}_{\mathcal {N} \geqslant 1} (\mathcal {N}- 1)^{1 / 2} F \Vert ^2_{\dot{\mathcal {H}}^1_0} . $$\end{document}E[‖D2F‖V˙⊗sH2]=‖1N⩾1(N-1)1/2F‖H˙012.

Proof

This is the only proof where we explicitly use the chaos expansion under \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu $$\end{document}μ, and it could be avoided with some additional work. The chaos expansion is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F = \sum _{n = 0}^{\infty } W_n (\varphi _n)$$\end{document}F=∑n=0∞Wn(φn) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi _n \in H^{\otimes n}_s$$\end{document}φn∈Hs⊗n. Then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E} [\Vert D^2 F \Vert _{\dot{V} \otimes _s H}^2]&= \sum _{k_1, k_2, \ell _1, \ell _2} \mathbb {E} \left[ D_{e_{\ell _1}} D_{e_{k_1}} F D_{e_{\ell _2}} D_{e_{k_2}} F \right] \langle e_{\ell _1}, e_{\ell _2} \rangle _{\dot{V}} \langle e_{k_1}, e_{k_2} \rangle _H\\&= \sum _{k, \ell _1, \ell _2} \sum _{n = 2}^{\infty } n^2 (n - 1)^2 \mathbb {E} [W_{n - 2} (\varphi _n (e_{\ell _1}, e_k, \cdot )) W_{n - 2} (\varphi _n (e_{\ell _2}, e_k, \cdot ))] \langle e_{\ell _1}, e_{\ell _2} \rangle _{\dot{V}}\\&= \sum _{k, \ell _1, \ell _2} \sum _{n = 2}^{\infty } n^2 (n - 1)^2 (n - 2) ! \langle \varphi _n (e_{\ell _1}, e_k, \cdot ), \varphi _n (e_{\ell _2}, e_k, \cdot ) \rangle _{H^{\otimes (n - 2)}_s} \langle e_{\ell _1}, e_{\ell _2} \rangle _{\dot{V}}\\&= \sum _{\ell _1, \ell _2} \sum _{n = 2}^{\infty } n^2 (n - 1) (n - 1) ! \langle \varphi _n (e_{\ell _1}, \cdot ), \varphi _n (e_{\ell _2}, \cdot ) \rangle _{H^{\otimes (n - 1)}_s} \langle e_{\ell _1}, e_{\ell _2} \rangle _{\dot{V}}\\&= \sum _{n = 1}^{\infty } (n - 1) \mathbb {E} \left[ D_{e_{\ell _1}} W_n (\varphi _n) D_{e_{\ell _2}} W_n (\varphi _n) \right] \langle e_{\ell _1}, e_{\ell _2} \rangle _{\dot{V}}\\&= \sum _{n = 1}^{\infty } \mathbb {E} \left[ D_{e_{\ell _1}} (\mathcal {N}- 1)^{1 / 2} W_n (\varphi _n) D_{e_{\ell _2}} (\mathcal {N}- 1)^{1 / 2} W_n (\varphi _n) \right] \langle e_{\ell _1}, e_{\ell _2} \rangle _{\dot{V}}\\&= \Vert \boldsymbol{1}_{\mathcal {N} \geqslant 1} (\mathcal {N}- 1)^{1 / 2} F \Vert ^2_{\dot{\mathcal {H}}^1_0} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[‖D2F‖V˙⊗sH2]=∑k1,k2,ℓ1,ℓ2EDeℓ1Dek1FDeℓ2Dek2F⟨eℓ1,eℓ2⟩V˙⟨ek1,ek2⟩H=∑k,ℓ1,ℓ2∑n=2∞n2(n-1)2E[Wn-2(φn(eℓ1,ek,·))Wn-2(φn(eℓ2,ek,·))]⟨eℓ1,eℓ2⟩V˙=∑k,ℓ1,ℓ2∑n=2∞n2(n-1)2(n-2)!⟨φn(eℓ1,ek,·),φn(eℓ2,ek,·)⟩Hs⊗(n-2)⟨eℓ1,eℓ2⟩V˙=∑ℓ1,ℓ2∑n=2∞n2(n-1)(n-1)!⟨φn(eℓ1,·),φn(eℓ2,·)⟩Hs⊗(n-1)⟨eℓ1,eℓ2⟩V˙=∑n=1∞(n-1)EDeℓ1Wn(φn)Deℓ2Wn(φn)⟨eℓ1,eℓ2⟩V˙=∑n=1∞EDeℓ1(N-1)1/2Wn(φn)Deℓ2(N-1)1/2Wn(φn)⟨eℓ1,eℓ2⟩V˙=‖1N⩾1(N-1)1/2F‖H˙012.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Our estimates for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {G}^{\rho }_+$$\end{document}G+ρ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {G}^{\rho }_-$$\end{document}G-ρ are based on duality arguments, which means that we need strong assumptions on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\rho _N)$$\end{document}(ρN) to obtain the strong convergence of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\mathcal {G}^{\rho _N} F)_N$$\end{document}(GρNF)N in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}^{- 1}_0$$\end{document}H0-1 in the next lemma. The weak convergence in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}^{- 1}_0$$\end{document}H0-1 would require much weaker assumptions, essentially only \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \rho _N h \rightarrow h$$\end{document}ρNh→h in H for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h\in H$$\end{document}h∈H, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _N v \rightarrow v$$\end{document}ρNv→v in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\dot{V}$$\end{document}V˙ for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v \in \dot{V}$$\end{document}v∈V˙.

Lemma 2.16

(Approximation of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {G}} F$$\end{document}GF) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\rho _N)_{N \in \mathbb {N}} \subset L (H, V)$$\end{document}(ρN)N∈N⊂L(H,V) be such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sup _N \Vert \rho _N \Vert _{L (H, H)} + \Vert \rho _N \Vert _{L (\dot{V}, \dot{V})} < \infty $$\end{document}supN‖ρN‖L(H,H)+‖ρN‖L(V˙,V˙)<∞ and for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f \in S$$\end{document}f∈S\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert (\rho _N-\text {Id}) f \Vert _V + \Vert \langle B\circ \rho _N^{\otimes 2}, f \rangle _{V^{*}, V} - Proof

By similar computations as in Lemma 2.14 we obtain for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G \in \mathcal {C}$$\end{document}G∈C44\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E} [(\mathcal {G}_+^{\rho _N} F -\mathcal {G}^{\rho _M}_+ F) G]&= \mathbb {E} [\langle B (\rho _N^{\otimes 2}D^2 (1 +\mathcal {N})^{- 1} G) - B (\rho _M^{\otimes 2}D^2 (1 +\mathcal {N})^{- 1} G), \nonumber \\&\quad \rho _N D (2 +\mathcal {N}) F \rangle _{\dot{V}^{*}, \dot{V}}] \nonumber \\&\quad + \mathbb {E} [\langle B (\rho _M^{\otimes 2}D^2 (1 +\mathcal {N})^{- 1} G), (\rho _N - \rho _M) D (2 +\mathcal {N}) F \rangle _{\dot{V}^{*}, \dot{V}}] \nonumber \\&\lesssim \mathbb {E} [(\varepsilon _N + \varepsilon _M) \Vert D^2 (1 +\mathcal {N})^{- 1} G \Vert _{V \otimes _s H} C_1 (D (2 +\mathcal {N}) F)] \nonumber \\&\leqslant (\varepsilon _N + \varepsilon _M) \Vert G \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^1_0} \mathbb {E} [C_1 (D (2 +\mathcal {N}) F)^2]^{1 / 2} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[(G+NF-G+MF)G]=E[⟨B(ρN⊗2D2(1+N)-1G)-B(ρM⊗2D2(1+N)-1G),ρND(2+N)F⟩V˙∗,V˙]+E[⟨B(ρM⊗2D2(1+N)-1G),(ρN-ρM)D(2+N)F⟩V˙∗,V˙]≲E[(εN+εM)‖D2(1+N)-1G‖V⊗sHC1(D(2+N)F)]⩽(εN+εM)‖G‖H01E[C1(D(2+N)F)2]1/2and analogously\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E} [(\mathcal {G}_-^{\rho _N} F -\mathcal {G}^{\rho _M}_- F) G]&\lesssim \mathbb {E} [(\varepsilon _N + \varepsilon _M) C_2 (D^2 F) \Vert D G \Vert _{\dot{V}}]\\&\leqslant (\varepsilon _N + \varepsilon _M) \mathbb {E} [C_2 (D^2 F)^2]^{1 / 2} \Vert G \Vert _{\dot{\mathcal {H}}^{1}_0}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[(G-NF-G-MF)G]≲E[(εN+εM)C2(D2F)‖DG‖V˙]⩽(εN+εM)E[C2(D2F)2]1/2‖G‖H˙01,which shows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\mathcal {G}^{\rho _N} F)_N$$\end{document}(GρNF)N is Cauchy in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}^{- 1}_0$$\end{document}H0-1 and the limit does not depend on the specific approximation, it satisfies the bounds of Lemma 2.14 and it is antisymmetric on cylinder functions.

The convergence of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\langle : \tilde{B}^{\rho _N} (\eta , \eta ):, f \rangle _{V^{*}, V} = \mathcal {G}^{\rho _N} (\eta (f)) + \langle {:} \tilde{B}^{\rho _N} (\eta , \eta ) {:}, f - \rho _N f \rangle _{V^{*}, V}$$\end{document}⟨:B~ρN(η,η):,f⟩V∗,V=GρN(η(f))+⟨:B~ρN(η,η):,f-ρNf⟩V∗,V is similar. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Remark 2.19

The uniform \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2 bound for the density and the energy estimate are formulated in a way that corresponds to energy solutions that at time 0 have a distribution with density with respect to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu $$\end{document}μ that is in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2(\mu )$$\end{document}L2(μ). It would be possible to relax this to an \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1$$\end{document}L1 density by replacing the control of moments by a control of probabilities, see [23, Def. 13].

In applications to scaling limits of particle systems the following characterization of energy solutions is sometimes useful.

Proof

There are two statements which are not obvious. First, that i.-iv. imply an improved version of the energy estimate, which is shown in Lemma 2.21 below, and which establishes that u is an energy solution. The lemma does not actually require the full strength of iii., and in particular the only requirement on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {B}}^f$$\end{document}Bf is that it is of vanishing quadratic variation.

Lemma 2.21 then allows us to show the second claim: Assume that the sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\rho _N)_{N\in {\mathbb {N}}}$$\end{document}(ρN)N∈N is as in Assumption (B). Then the convergence of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\langle {:} {\tilde{B}}^{\rho _N} (\eta , \eta ) {:}, f \rangle _{V^{*}, V}$$\end{document}⟨:B~ρN(η,η):,f⟩V∗,V to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\langle {:} B (\eta , \eta ) {:}, f \rangle _{V^{*}, V}$$\end{document}⟨:B(η,η):,f⟩V∗,V in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}^{-1}_0$$\end{document}H0-1 follows from Lemma 2.16. If in point iii. the process \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {B}}^f$$\end{document}Bf is of vanishing quadratic variation and we have uniform convergence on compacts of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\int _0^\cdot \langle {:} {\tilde{B}}^{\rho _N} (u_s, u_s) {:}, f \rangle _{V^{*}, V} \textrm{d}s$$\end{document}∫0·⟨:B~ρN(us,us):,f⟩V∗,Vds to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {B}}^f$$\end{document}Bf, we obtain the improved energy estimate (49) by Lemma 2.21. It remains to show that the convergence\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \int _0^t\cdot \langle {:} {\tilde{B}}^{\rho _N} (u_s, u_s) {:}, f \rangle _{V^{*}, V} \textrm{d}s \rightarrow {\mathcal {B}}^f $$\end{document}∫0t·⟨:B~ρN(us,us):,f⟩V∗,Vds→Bfholds in p-variation for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p<2$$\end{document}p<2. By the Besov-variation embedding shown in Corollary A.3 of the appendix of [14] it suffices to show that, with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I_{s,t} = I_t - I_s$$\end{document}Is,t=It-Is and for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p<2$$\end{document}p<2,48\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{N\rightarrow \infty } \int _{[0,T]^2}\frac{{\mathbb {E}}[|I(\langle {:} {\tilde{B}}^{\rho _N} (\cdot , \cdot ) {:}, f \rangle _{V^{*}, V} - \langle {:} B (\cdot , \cdot ) {:}, f \rangle _{V^{*}, V})_{s,t}|^2]}{|t-s|^{1+2/p}}\textrm{d}s \textrm{d}t =0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limN→∞∫[0,T]2E[|I(⟨:B~ρN(·,·):,f⟩V∗,V-⟨:B(·,·):,f⟩V∗,V)s,t|2]|t-s|1+2/pdsdt=0.Shifting (49) to the interval [s, t] by stationarity and ignoring the higher order contribution \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t-s)^2$$\end{document}(t-s)2 because we are interested in short length scales, we obtain from the improved energy estimate and (43) for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M \in {\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}M∈N\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {\mathbb {E}}[|I(\langle {:} {\tilde{B}}^{\rho _M} (\cdot , \cdot ) {:}, f \rangle _{V^{*}, V} - The proof used the following lemma, which might be useful in its own right.

Lemma 2.21

We make Assumption (A) and additionally assume that S is a core for A. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_t (f))_{t \geqslant 0, f \in S}$$\end{document}(ut(f))t⩾0,f∈S be an adapted process which satisfies conditions i., ii., iv. from Theorem 2.20 and also iii.’For each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f\in S$$\end{document}f∈S there exists a process \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {B}}^f$$\end{document}Bf of vanishing quadratic variation such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} M^f_t :=u_t (f) - u_0 (f) + \int _0^t u_s(Af)\textrm{d}s - {\mathcal {B}}^f_t, \qquad t \geqslant 0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Mtf:=ut(f)-u0(f)+∫0tus(Af)ds-Btf,t⩾0, is a continuous martingale with quadratic variation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[M^f]_t = 2t \langle A f, f \rangle _H$$\end{document}[Mf]t=2t⟨Af,f⟩H.Then u satisfies the improved energy estimate49\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E} \left[ \sup _{t \leqslant T} \left| \int _0^t F (u_s) \textrm{d}s \right| ^2 \right] \lesssim (T + T^2) \Vert F \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^{- 1}_0}^2,\qquad F\in {\mathcal {C}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Esupt⩽T∫0tF(us)ds2≲(T+T2)‖F‖H0-12,F∈C.

Proof

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F \in \mathcal {C}$$\end{document}F∈C we obtain from the Itô trick (we can apply Itô’s formula for Dirichlet processes by the assumption that the drift has vanishing quadratic variation, together with the usual time reversal argument as in [20]) that50\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E} \left[ \sup _{t \leqslant T} \left| \int _0^t \mathcal {L}_0 F (u_s) \textrm{d}s \right| ^2 \right] \lesssim T \Vert F \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^1_0}^2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Esupt⩽T∫0tL0F(us)ds2≲T‖F‖H012.By Lemma A.4 in the appendix, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {C}$$\end{document}C is a core for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_0$$\end{document}L0. Therefore, the bound (50) extends to all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F \in \mathcal {D} (\mathcal {L}_0)$$\end{document}F∈D(L0): Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(F^N)_N \subset \mathcal {C}$$\end{document}(FN)N⊂C be such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F^N \rightarrow F$$\end{document}FN→F and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_0 F^N \rightarrow \mathcal {L}_0 F$$\end{document}L0FN→L0F. (56) we have 58\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert F^{\sharp } \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^{- 1}_0} \Vert F^N \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^1_0} \geqslant \langle F^{\sharp }, F^N \rangle _{\mathcal {H}^{- 1}_0, \mathcal {H}^1_0} = K^N (F^N, F^N ) \simeq \Vert F^N \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^1_0}^2, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖F♯‖H0-1‖FN‖H01⩾⟨F♯,FN⟩H0-1,H01=KN(FN,FN)≃‖FN‖H012, uniformly in N, so that 59\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert F^N \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^1_0} \lesssim \Vert F^{\sharp } \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^{- 1}_0} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖FN‖H01≲‖F♯‖H0-1.

By eq. (56) we have 58\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert F^{\sharp } \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^{- 1}_0} \Vert F^N \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^1_0} \geqslant \langle F^{\sharp }, F^N \rangle _{\mathcal {H}^{- 1}_0, \mathcal {H}^1_0} = K^N (F^N, F^N ) \simeq \Vert F^N \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^1_0}^2, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖F♯‖H0-1‖FN‖H01⩾⟨F♯,FN⟩H0-1,H01=KN(FN,FN)≃‖FN‖H012, uniformly in N, so that 59\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert F^N \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^1_0} \lesssim \Vert F^{\sharp } \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^{- 1}_0} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖FN‖H01≲‖F♯‖H0-1.

With the weak convergence, this follows by testing against \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G \in \mathcal {C}$$\end{document}G∈C and applying the dominated convergence theorem and using that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G \in \mathcal {H}^1_1$$\end{document}G∈H11 to obtain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert (\mathcal {G} - \mathcal {G}^N) G \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^{- 1}_0} \rightarrow 0$$\end{document}‖(G-GN)G‖H0-1→0. See [33, Prop. 1.2] or [22, Prop. 4.9] for details.

Lemma 3.2

It holds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \langle (1 -\mathcal {L}) F, F \rangle _{L^2 (\mu )} \simeq \Vert F \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^1_0}^2, \qquad F \in \mathcal {D}_{\max } = \{ F \in \mathcal {H}^1_0 : \mathcal {L} F \in L^2 (\mu ) \} \subset \mathcal {H}^1_0 . $$\end{document}⟨(1-L)F,F⟩L2(μ)≃‖F‖H012,F∈Dmax={F∈H01:LF∈L2(μ)}⊂H01.Consequently, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(1 -\mathcal {L})$$\end{document}(1-L) is injective on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}_{\max }$$\end{document}Dmax,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (1 - \mathcal {L}) \mathcal {C} \subset \mathcal {H}^{- 1}_0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(1-L)C⊂H0-1,is dense in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}^{- 1}_0$$\end{document}H0-1 and the domain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}$$\end{document}D from the previous Proposition 3.1 is equal to the maximal domain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}_{\max }$$\end{document}Dmax.

Proof

The proof is based on the commutator estimate (30) and can be found in e.g. [33, Thm. 1.4] or [22, Thm. 4.11]. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Construction of energy solutions

In this section we assume that there exists a complete orthonormal system \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(e_n)_{n \in \mathbb {N}} \subset V$$\end{document}(en)n∈N⊂V with respect to H of eigenvectors for A with an increasing sequence of eigenvalues \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\lambda _n)_{n \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(λn)n∈N, which morally corresponds to the case that V is compactly embedded in H. This assumption could be bypassed by a more complex approximation argument, involving a natural discretization of the spectrum of A that leads to an H-bounded perturbation of this operator, but for simplicity we restrict our attention to the special case where A can be diagonalized. Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _n \geqslant 0$$\end{document}λn⩾0 for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}n∈N because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\langle A f, f \rangle _H \geqslant c \Vert f \Vert _{\dot{V}}^2$$\end{document}⟨Af,f⟩H⩾c‖f‖V˙2. We also assume that S is a nuclear Fréchet space.

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}N∈N. Consider the projected SDE corresponding to equation (11), for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k = 1, \ldots , N$$\end{document}k=1,…,N,60\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textrm{d}u^N_t (e_k) = \left( - \lambda _k u^N_t (e_k) + \langle {:}B^N(u^N_t,u^N_t){:}, e_k\rangle _{V^*, V} \right) \textrm{d}t + \sqrt{2 \lambda _k} \textrm{d}W^k_t, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}dutN(ek)=-λkutN(ek)+⟨:BN(utN,utN):,ek⟩V∗,Vdt+2λkdWtk,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(W^k)_k$$\end{document}(Wk)k are i.i.d. Brownian motions and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {:}B^N (u, u){:} :=\sum _{k, \ell , m \leqslant N} (u (e_{\ell }) u (e_m) - \delta _{\ell , m}) \langle B (e_{\ell }, e_m), e_k \rangle _{V^{*}, V} e_k. $$\end{document}:BN(u,u)::=∑k,ℓ,m⩽N(u(eℓ)u(em)-δℓ,m)⟨B(eℓ,em),ek⟩V∗,Vek.We claim that an invariant probability measure is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ (u^N_k)^N_{k = 1} : = (u^N (e_k))_{k = 1}^N \sim \mu _N =\mathcal {N} (0,\mathbb {I}_{N \times N}) . $$\end{document}(ukN)k=1N:=(uN(ek))k=1N∼μN=N(0,IN×N).Indeed, the generator of the SDE is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathcal {L}^N = \sum _{k = 1}^N \left( \lambda _k (- u^N_k \partial _k + \partial _{k k}) + \sum _{\ell , m \leqslant N} (u^N_{\ell } u^N_m - \delta _{\ell , m}) \langle B (e_{\ell }, e_m), e_k \rangle _{V^{*}, V} \partial _k \right) . $$\end{document}LN=∑k=1Nλk(-ukN∂k+∂kk)+∑ℓ,m⩽N(uℓNumN-δℓ,m)⟨B(eℓ,em),ek⟩V∗,V∂k.It can be split up into a symmetric part\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathcal {L}_0^N = \sum _{k = 1}^N \lambda _k (- u^N_k \partial _k + \partial _{k k}), $$\end{document}L0N=∑k=1Nλk(-ukN∂k+∂kk),which is the generator of an Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process in N dimensions and hence preserves \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _N$$\end{document}μN, whereas by analogous reasoning as in Lemma 2.7 the contribution of the drift\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathcal {G}^N = \sum _{k, \ell , m \leqslant N} (u^N_{\ell } u^N_m - \delta _{\ell , m}) \langle B (e_{\ell }, e_m), e_k \rangle _{V^{*}, V} \partial _k, $$\end{document}GN=∑k,ℓ,m⩽N(uℓNumN-δℓ,m)⟨B(eℓ,em),ek⟩V∗,V∂k,is antisymmetric and hence the full generator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}^N$$\end{document}LN has \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _N$$\end{document}μN as an invariant measure. We start \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^N$$\end{document}uN in its invariant measure (and discuss relaxed assumptions later), and we test against \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f \in H$$\end{document}f∈H via\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} u^N_t (f) = \sum _{k = 1}^N u^N_t (e_k) \langle e_k, f \rangle _H . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}utN(f)=∑k=1NutN(ek)⟨ek,f⟩H.Let us first derive an energy estimate for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^N$$\end{document}uN, uniformly in N. We define the projection61\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Pi _N h = \sum _{k=1}^N \langle h,e_k\rangle _H e_k, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ΠNh=∑k=1N⟨h,ek⟩Hek,and for a cylinder function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F(\eta )=\Phi (\eta (f))$$\end{document}F(η)=Φ(η(f)) we define a new cylinder function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Pi _N F(\eta ) = \Phi (\eta (\Pi _N f))$$\end{document}ΠNF(η)=Φ(η(ΠNf)).

Lemma 3.3

(Itô trick and energy estimate) For any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} Proof

We assume without loss of generality that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F=\Pi _N F$$\end{document}F=ΠNF; otherwise we use that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F=\Pi _N F$$\end{document}F=ΠNF \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu ^N$$\end{document}μN-a.s. and derive the estimate for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Pi _N F$$\end{document}ΠNF in place of F. By time reversal of Markov processes, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^N$$\end{document}uN is an energy solution for the projected equation (60) with non-linearity \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B^N$$\end{document}BN in the sense of Lemma 2.20. Therefore, the same arguments as in [20, Proposition 3.2] show that for any cylinder function F\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ 2 \int _0^t \mathcal {L}_0^N F (u_s^N) \textrm{d}s = M_t^F + \hat{M}^F_{T - t} - \hat{M}^F_T, $$\end{document}2∫0tL0NF(usN)ds=MtF+M^T-tF-M^TF,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{d}M^F_t = \sum _{k = 1}^N \partial _k F (u^N_t) \sqrt{2 \lambda _k} \textrm{d}W^k_t$$\end{document}dMtF=∑k=1N∂kF(utN)2λkdWtk and thus \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \textrm{d}[M^F]_t = 2 \sum _{k = 1}^N \lambda _k | \partial _k F (u^N_t) |^2, $$\end{document}d[MF]t=2∑k=1Nλk|∂kF(utN)|2, and similarly for the backward martingale \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{M}^F$$\end{document}M^F. The Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality now yields\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathbb {E} \left[ \sup _{t \leqslant T} \left| \int _0^t \mathcal {L}_0^N F (u_s^N) \textrm{d}s \right| ^p \right] \lesssim T^{p / 2} \left\| \sum _{k = 1}^N \lambda _k | \partial _k F |^2 \right\| _{L^{p / 2} (\mu _N)}^{p / 2}, $$\end{document}Esupt⩽T∫0tL0NF(usN)dsp≲Tp/2∑k=1Nλk|∂kF|2Lp/2(μN)p/2,and for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p = 2$$\end{document}p=2 the right hand side is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ T \sum _{k = 1}^N \lambda _k \Vert \partial _k F \Vert _{L^2 (\mu _N)}^2 = T \langle F, (-\mathcal {L}_0^N) F \rangle _{L^2 (\mu ^N)} . $$\end{document}T∑k=1Nλk‖∂kF‖L2(μN)2=T⟨F,(-L0N)F⟩L2(μN).If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} $$\end{document}∑k=1Nλk|∂kF|2Lp/2(μN)p/2≃M⟨F,(-L0N)F⟩L2(μN)p/2.We can of course also bound \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} Lemma 3.4

For all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f \in V$$\end{document}f∈V the processes \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u^N (f))_{N \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(uN(f))N∈N are uniformly tight in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C({\mathbb {R}}_+, {\mathbb {R}})$$\end{document}C(R+,R), and there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p<2$$\end{document}p<2 such that also the real-valued random variables \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\Vert \int _0^\cdot \langle {:}B^N (u^N_r, u^N_r){:}, f \rangle _{V^{*}, V} \textrm{d}r \Vert _{[0,T],p-var})_{N}$$\end{document}(‖∫0·⟨:BN(urN,urN):,f⟩V∗,Vdr‖[0,T],p-var)N are uniformly tight for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T>0$$\end{document}T>0.

Proof

Tightness at the initial time is clear, because\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E} [u^N_0 (f)^2] = \Vert \Pi _N f \Vert _H^2 \leqslant \Vert f \Vert _H^2 . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[u0N(f)2]=‖ΠNf‖H2⩽‖f‖H2.For the time increment \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^N_{s,t}(f) = u^N_t(f) - u^N_s(f)$$\end{document}us,tN(f)=utN(f)-usN(f) we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} u^N_{s, t} (f)&= \int _s^t u^N_r (- A \Pi _N f) \textrm{d}r + \int _s^t \langle {:}B^N (u^N_r, u^N_r){:}, f \rangle _{V^{*}, V} \textrm{d}r + M^{f, N}_{s, t}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}us,tN(f)=∫sturN(-AΠNf)dr+∫st⟨:BN(urN,urN):,f⟩V∗,Vdr+Ms,tf,N.The quadratic variation of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M^{f,N}$$\end{document}Mf,N is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&[M^{f, N}]_{s,t} = 2 (t-s) \sum _{k = 1}^N \lambda _k \langle f, e_k \rangle _H^2 \leqslant 2 (t-s) \sum _{k = 1}^\infty \lambda _k \langle f, e_k \rangle _H^2\\&\quad = 2 (t-s) \langle A f, f \rangle _H \simeq (t-s) \Vert f \Vert _{\dot{V}}^2, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}[Mf,N]s,t=2(t-s)∑k=1Nλk⟨f,ek⟩H2⩽2(t-s)∑k=1∞λk⟨f,ek⟩H2=2(t-s)⟨Af,f⟩H≃(t-s)‖f‖V˙2,which gives tightness for the martingale. Also, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u (A \Pi _N f) =\mathcal {L}_0^N u (f)$$\end{document}u(AΠNf)=L0Nu(f) and thus by the Itô trick (62)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \int _s^t u^N_r (- A \Pi _N f) \textrm{d}r \right| ^p \right] \lesssim | t - s |^{p / 2} \sum _{k = 1}^N \lambda _k \langle f, e_k \rangle _H^2 \\&\quad \leqslant | t - s |^{p / 2} \Vert f \Vert _{\dot{V}}^2, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E∫sturN(-AΠNf)drp≲|t-s|p/2∑k=1Nλk⟨f,ek⟩H2⩽|t-s|p/2‖f‖V˙2,which yields tightness for the linear contribution to the drift. It remains to treat the nonlinear part of the drift. Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\langle {:}B^N(\eta ,\eta ){:}, f\rangle = {\mathcal {G}}^{\Pi _N} \eta (f)$$\end{document}⟨:BN(η,η):,f⟩=GΠNη(f) is a second order polynomial in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta $$\end{document}η, and therefore the hypercontractive version of the energy estimate, (63), together with the bounds for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {G}^{\Pi _N}$$\end{document}GΠN from (39) yields64\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\mathbb {E}\left[ \left| \int _s^t\langle {:}B^N(u^N_r,u^N_r){:},\Pi _Nf\rangle _H\textrm{d}r\right| ^p\right] \nonumber \\&\quad \lesssim |t-s|^{p/2}\left\| {\mathcal {G}}^{\Pi _N} \eta (f)\right\| _{\mathcal {H}^{-1}_0}^2 \nonumber \\&\quad \lesssim |t-s|^{p/2}(\Vert \Pi _N \Vert _{L (H, H)} + \Vert \Pi _N \Vert _{L (\dot{V}, \dot{V})})^3 \Vert \eta (f) \Vert _{\mathcal {H}^1_{1}} \nonumber \\&\quad \simeq |t-s|^{p/2}(\Vert \Pi _N \Vert _{L (H, H)} + \Vert \Pi _N \Vert _{L (\dot{V}, \dot{V})})^3 \Vert f \Vert _{V}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E∫st⟨:BN(urN,urN):,ΠNf⟩Hdrp≲|t-s|p/2GΠNη(f)H0-12≲|t-s|p/2(‖ΠN‖L(H,H)+‖ΠN‖L(V˙,V˙))3‖η(f)‖H11≃|t-s|p/2(‖ΠN‖L(H,H)+‖ΠN‖L(V˙,V˙))3‖f‖V,uniformly in N. Thus, we obtain tightness as soon as we show that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \Pi _N \Vert _{L (H, H)} + \Vert \Pi _N \Vert _{L (\dot{V}, \dot{V})}\lesssim 1$$\end{document}‖ΠN‖L(H,H)+‖ΠN‖L(V˙,V˙)≲1. The first term is clearly bounded by 1 because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Pi _N$$\end{document}ΠN is an orthogonal projection in H. For the second term we use that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(e_k)$$\end{document}(ek) are eigenvectors for A to bound\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \Vert \Pi _N f \Vert _{\dot{V}}^2 \simeq \langle A \Pi _N f, \Pi _N f\rangle _{V^*, V} \leqslant \langle A f, f\rangle _{V^*, V} \simeq \Vert f\Vert _{\dot{V}}^2, $$\end{document}‖ΠNf‖V˙2≃⟨AΠNf,ΠNf⟩V∗,V⩽⟨Af,f⟩V∗,V≃‖f‖V˙2,and this concludes the proof of tightness of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u^N)$$\end{document}(uN). Tightness for the p-variation is shown with the same argument as in Lemma 2.20. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Theorem 3.5

(Existence of energy solutions) We make Assumption 1 and 1 and we assume that there exists an orthonormal basis of eigenvectors for A and that S is a nuclear Fréchet space. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu \ll \mu $$\end{document}ν≪μ be such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{\textrm{d}\nu }{\textrm{d}\mu } \in L^2(\mu )$$\end{document}dνdμ∈L2(μ). Then there exists an energy solution u with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_0 \sim \nu $$\end{document}u0∼ν.

Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {F}}_N = \sigma (\eta (e_1), \dots , \eta (e_N))$$\end{document}FN=σ(η(e1),⋯,η(eN)) and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma ^N = \mathbb {E}[\textrm{d}\nu / \textrm{d}\mu |{\mathcal {F}}_N]$$\end{document}γN=E[dν/dμ|FN] (conditional expectation in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2(\mu )$$\end{document}L2(μ)) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{d}\nu ^N = \gamma ^N \textrm{d}\mu ^N$$\end{document}dνN=γNdμN. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^N$$\end{document}uN be a solution to the SDE (60) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^N_0\sim \nu ^N$$\end{document}u0N∼νN. We claim that the sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u^N)_{N \in {\mathbb {N}}}$$\end{document}(uN)N∈N is uniformly tight in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C({\mathbb {R}}_+, S')$$\end{document}C(R+,S′) and that any limit point u is an energy solution with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_0 \sim \nu $$\end{document}u0∼ν.

To show tightness of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u^N)_N$$\end{document}(uN)N in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C({\mathbb {R}}_+, S')$$\end{document}C(R+,S′) we apply Mitoma’s criterion [31], which states that it suffices to show tightness of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u^N(f))_N$$\end{document}(uN(f))N in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C({\mathbb {R}}_+, {\mathbb {R}})$$\end{document}C(R+,R) for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f \in S$$\end{document}f∈S; this is the only part of the argument where we use that S is a nuclear Fréchet space. Lemma 3.4 gives us the tightness of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u^N(f))_N$$\end{document}(uN(f))N if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu =\mu $$\end{document}ν=μ. Otherwise, we perform a change of measure at the initial time and we apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Jensen’s inequality: For any bounded and measurable \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi : C({\mathbb {R}}_+, {\mathbb {R}}) \rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}Ψ:C(R+,R)→R65\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E}_{\nu ^N}[\Psi (u^N)]&= \int \frac{\textrm{d}\nu ^N}{\textrm{d}\mu ^N}(u) \mathbb {E}_{u}[\Psi (u^N)] \mu ^N(\textrm{d}u)\nonumber \\&\leqslant \left\| \frac{\textrm{d}\nu ^N}{\textrm{d}\mu ^N} \right\| _{L^2(\mu ^N)} \left( \int \mathbb {E}_{u}[\Psi (u^N)]^2 \mu ^N(\textrm{d}u)\right) ^{1/2} \nonumber \\&\leqslant \left\| \frac{\textrm{d}\nu }{\textrm{d}\mu } \right\| _{L^2(\mu )} \mathbb {E}_{\mu ^N}[\Psi (u^N)^2]^{1/2}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}EνN[Ψ(uN)]=∫dνNdμN(u)Eu[Ψ(uN)]μN(du)⩽dνNdμNL2(μN)∫Eu[Ψ(uN)]2μN(du)1/2⩽dνdμL2(μ)EμN[Ψ(uN)2]1/2.Tightness follows by taking \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi $$\end{document}Ψ as the indicator function of the complement of a compact set K in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C({\mathbb {R}}_+, {\mathbb {R}})$$\end{document}C(R+,R) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sup _N \mathbb {P}_{\mu ^N}(u^N(f) \in K^c) \leqslant \varepsilon $$\end{document}supNPμN(uN(f)∈Kc)⩽ε.

\langle {:}{\tilde{B}}^{\rho _M}(u^N_s,u^N_s){:}, f\rangle _{V^*, V}\nonumber \\&\quad = \langle {:}B(\Pi _N u^N_s,\Pi _N u^N_s){:}, \Pi _N f - f\rangle _{V^*, V} \nonumber \\&\qquad + \langle {:}B(\Pi _N u^N_s,\Pi _N u^N_s){:} -{:}{\tilde{B}}^{\rho _M}(\Pi _N u^N_s,\Pi _N u^N_s){:}, f\rangle _{V^*, V}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}⟨:BN(usN,usN):,f⟩V∗,V-⟨:B~ρM(usN,usN):,f⟩V∗,V=⟨:B(ΠNusN,ΠNusN):,ΠNf-f⟩V∗,V+⟨:B(ΠNusN,ΠNusN):-:B~ρM(ΠNusN,ΠNusN):,f⟩V∗,V.For the first term on the right hand side we simply bound with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\mathbb {E}_{\mu ^N}\left[ \left| \int _s^t \langle {:}B(\Pi _N u^N_r,\Pi _N u^N_r){:}, \Pi _N f -
